========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I wouldn't say I set out with a specific goal to lose all this weight, honestly. It just kind of happened as I was trying to be more disciplined and eat cleaner, you know? Just trying to make good choices. 

But, um... to answer the second part of your question... I guess the idea of gaining weight does make me feel a little... anxious? Well, maybe more than a little. It's actually really scary to think about. Like, if I were to eat a regular dinner, or something cooked with oil or butter, I just... I get this sudden panic. I feel like if I gain even one pound, it'll show immediately. Like everyone will look at me and know that I slipped up, that I lost all my self-control. 

I just really want to be seen as someone who has it together. It's normal to want to maintain the progress you've made, right? I hope it doesn't make me sound crazy that it scares me so much.

Um... I mean, I wouldn't say "frightening," exactly. That sounds so... extreme, right? I just... I really like being in control of things.

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or exercised excessively after eating because you were worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, I don't do anything like that. I promise. I know some people do, but I... I really try to just stick to my routine, you know? \n\nI do walk around campus a lot, but that's just because I have classes all over and I like to stay active. It's just normal college stuff, right? I'd never do anything... extreme. I just try to be careful and responsible with my choices. I hope that's the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Do you base how you feel about yourself a lot on your body weight or shape?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? But I think that's pretty normal for girls my age, right? \n\nI just... I like to feel like I'm presenting myself well. Like, um, recently there was this showcase for my art classes, and we took some pictures. And I noticed that when I look at those photos, I don't really look at the artwork first. I kind of... I zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough, it just makes me feel like I'm, you know, serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too vain. I just really want to make sure I'm projecting the right image, if that makes sense? Is it okay that I feel that way?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently, and over what period of time did this happen?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'm not entirely sure of the exact number, honestly. I try not to obsess over the scale too much. But, um, I guess I have lost some weight over the last few months? \n\nIt's actually why I'm here, I think. The student health center sent me because they said my weight was too low to clear me to help out with the summer youth art workshops. They said the number has just kept dropping since my freshman physical. But... to me, it really just feels like what happens when I'm super focused on my classes and just, you know, being busy and productive. I'm just trying to be a good student and stay on top of everything. \n\nI really didn't mean to cause any trouble or make anyone worry. Do you think they'll still let me do the workshops if I just promise to keep being healthy?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the weight you’ve lost was something you intentionally wanted to happen, and does the idea of gaining weight cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I wouldn't say I set out with a specific goal to lose all this weight, honestly. It just kind of happened as I was trying to be more disciplined and eat cleaner, you know? Just trying to make good choices. \n\nBut, um... to answer the second part of your question... I guess the idea of gaining weight does make me feel a little... anxious? Well, maybe more than a little. It's actually really scary to think about. Like, if I were to eat a regular dinner, or something cooked with oil or butter, I just... I get this sudden panic. I feel like if I gain even one pound, it'll show immediately. Like everyone will look at me and know that I slipped up, that I lost all my self-control. \n\nI just really want to be seen as someone who has it together. It's normal to want to maintain the progress you've made, right? I hope it doesn't make me sound crazy that it scares me so much.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on eating habits, weight, or shape started to interfere with your schoolwork, relationships, or other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard not to let it affect anything important. I mean, my grades are still really good! I always make sure my schoolwork gets done first. \n\nBut, um... I guess maybe it has gotten a little complicated recently? Like I mentioned before, the student health center... they won't clear me to lead the summer youth art workshops right now. Which is really disappointing, because I worked so hard to get that position after the showcase. But instead of getting to participate and help the kids, it's just turned into all these meetings and referrals... like this one. \n\nI just feel so bad that I'm letting people down. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. Do you think if I just keep showing up to these appointments and doing what I'm supposed to, they'll see that I'm trying and let me do the workshops? I just want everything to be okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, losing interest in things you used to enjoy, or having trouble sleeping or eating outside of your focus on healthy eating and weight?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I really try to keep a positive attitude about everything. I definitely haven't lost interest in the things I love—like, art is still my favorite thing in the world! That's why I'm so desperate to get cleared for the youth workshops. \n\nAnd, um, as for sleep... I think I sleep pretty normally? I kind of just... zoom right in on my arms and my waist. \n\nAnd, um, if I look small enough in the pictures, I just feel so much better about myself. Um... well, people do tell me that I'm too thin, like my mom and some of my friends. They get really worried and say it's dangerous, which... I mean, I appreciate that they care, I really do!

Um... well, didn't we just talk about this a little bit? About the art showcase photos? 

I mean, I don't think it's like, the *only* thing I judge myself on. I care about my grades and my art, too! But... I guess if I feel like I look a little heavier or softer one day, it does kind of ruin my mood. It just makes me feel like I'm failing at being disciplined, you know? Like I'm letting myself go.

I really want to do whatever I need to do to get cleared by them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight or change your eating habits in any way because of concerns about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I have changed how I eat a little bit? But I've mostly just been trying to be really healthy and eat clean, you know? I hope that's okay. \n\nI just... I get a little anxious about it, honestly. Like, if I'm being completely honest—and I know this probably sounds silly, I'm sorry—but even just the idea of gaining a single pound makes me kind of... panic? It just feels like if I were to sit down and eat a normal dinner, or, um, have something with butter in it, everyone would instantly be able to look at me and see that I’ve completely lost my discipline. I just really want to do things right and stay focused. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or exercised excessively after eating because you were worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, I don't do anything like that. I promise. I know some people do, but I... I really try to just stick to my routine, you know? \n\nI do walk around campus a lot, but that's just because I have classes all over and I like to stay active. It's just normal college stuff, right? I'd never do anything... extreme. I just try to be careful and responsible with my choices. I hope that's the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Do you base how you feel about yourself a lot on your body weight or shape?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? But I think that's pretty normal for girls my age, right? \n\nI just... I like to feel like I'm presenting myself well. Like, um, recently there was this showcase for my art classes, and we took some pictures. And I noticed that when I look at those photos, I don't really look at the artwork first. I kind of... I zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough, it just makes me feel like I'm, you know, serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too vain. I just really want to make sure I'm projecting the right image, if that makes sense? Is it okay that I feel that way?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently, and over what period of time did this happen?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'm not entirely sure of the exact number, honestly. I try not to obsess over the scale too much. But, um, I guess I have lost some weight over the last few months? \n\nIt's actually why I'm here, I think. The student health center sent me because they said my weight was too low to clear me to help out with the summer youth art workshops. They said the number has just kept dropping since my freshman physical. But... to me, it really just feels like what happens when I'm super focused on my classes and just, you know, being busy and productive. I'm just trying to be a good student and stay on top of everything. \n\nI really didn't mean to cause any trouble or make anyone worry. Do you think they'll still let me do the workshops if I just promise to keep being healthy?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the weight you’ve lost was something you intentionally wanted to happen, and does the idea of gaining weight cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I wouldn't say I set out with a specific goal to lose all this weight, honestly. It just kind of happened as I was trying to be more disciplined and eat cleaner, you know? Just trying to make good choices. \n\nBut, um... to answer the second part of your question... I guess the idea of gaining weight does make me feel a little... anxious? Well, maybe more than a little. It's actually really scary to think about. Like, if I were to eat a regular dinner, or something cooked with oil or butter, I just... I get this sudden panic. I feel like if I gain even one pound, it'll show immediately. Like everyone will look at me and know that I slipped up, that I lost all my self-control. \n\nI just really want to be seen as someone who has it together. It's normal to want to maintain the progress you've made, right? I hope it doesn't make me sound crazy that it scares me so much.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on eating habits, weight, or shape started to interfere with your schoolwork, relationships, or other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard not to let it affect anything important. I mean, my grades are still really good! I always make sure my schoolwork gets done first. \n\nBut, um... I guess maybe it has gotten a little complicated recently? Like I mentioned before, the student health center... they won't clear me to lead the summer youth art workshops right now. Which is really disappointing, because I worked so hard to get that position after the showcase. But instead of getting to participate and help the kids, it's just turned into all these meetings and referrals... like this one. \n\nI just feel so bad that I'm letting people down. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. Do you think if I just keep showing up to these appointments and doing what I'm supposed to, they'll see that I'm trying and let me do the workshops? I just want everything to be okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, losing interest in things you used to enjoy, or having trouble sleeping or eating outside of your focus on healthy eating and weight?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I really try to keep a positive attitude about everything. I definitely haven't lost interest in the things I love—like, art is still my favorite thing in the world! That's why I'm so desperate to get cleared for the youth workshops. \n\nAnd, um, as for sleep... I think I sleep pretty normally? I mean, I stay up late studying sometimes, but that's just regular college stuff, right? I don't have trouble falling asleep or anything. I usually just get regular-tired from trying to be productive all day. \n\nI really don't feel depressed or down... I'm still just me, you know? I just like to stay focused and work hard. That's a good sign, right? I really am trying my best to do everything right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent illnesses, digestive issues, or started any new medications that might affect your appetite or weight?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't been sick at all! I actually feel like I've been really healthy lately, probably because I've been so careful about what I eat, you know? Just trying to take good care of myself. \n\nAnd, um, no digestive issues or anything like that. I'm not taking any new medications either. Just my regular vitamins! I try to make sure I'm doing everything I'm supposed to do to stay healthy. \n\nI really don't think there's any physical reason for... well, for any of this. I'm just trying to be disciplined. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope I'm being helpful by answering all this. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I can get cleared for the workshops.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts about your body weight or shape strongly affect how you see yourself overall, or influence your mood and sense of self-worth?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think that's probably pretty normal for most girls my age, right? We all just want to look our best. \n\nThough, um... well, I probably shouldn't admit this, because it sounds a little silly, but... when I look at the photos from the art showcase we had recently, I don't really see the artwork first. I kind of just... zoom right in on my arms and my waist. \n\nAnd, um, if I look small enough in the pictures, I just feel so much better about myself. It makes me feel like I'm actually serious and put-together, you know? Like I'm disciplined and doing a good job with my life. It's really just about wanting to present myself well and show that I'm trying hard. I hope that doesn't sound too vain or anything? I just really care about doing things right.\n\nDoctor: Even though your weight is already quite low, does the thought of gaining even a small amount of weight feel extremely frightening or unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I wouldn't say \"frightening,\" exactly. That sounds so... extreme, right? I just... I really like being in control of things. \n\nBut, um... well, to be completely honest... the idea of gaining weight does make me a little anxious. Like, if I even think about gaining a pound, or if I have to eat a normal dinner, or... or something that has butter in it... I kind of start to panic. \n\nI just get this feeling that if I do that, everyone will instantly be able to see it. Like they'll look at me and know that I've lost my discipline and that I'm not trying hard anymore. And I really, really want people to know I'm a hard worker. That's understandable, isn't it? I just want to keep doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are overweight or not thin enough, even though others may tell you that you are already underweight?\n\nPatient: Um... well, people do tell me that I'm too thin, like my mom and some of my friends. They get really worried and say it's dangerous, which... I mean, I appreciate that they care, I really do! \n\nBut... honestly, I feel like they're kind of exaggerating? Like, when I look in the mirror, I definitely don't see someone who's dangerously thin. I just see areas where I could still be a little more toned, a little more disciplined. \n\nIt feels like they're making my body into this huge problem when really, I'm just trying to stay sharp and focused for school and my art. Being lighter just helps me feel more energized and clear-headed for all my work. That's a good thing, right? I'm just trying to do my best, and I don't think I'm doing anything as extreme as they make it sound. I hope you can see where I'm coming from?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your weight or shape has a strong impact on how you judge yourself overall, like whether you feel good or bad about who you are?\n\nPatient: Um... well, didn't we just talk about this a little bit? About the art showcase photos? \n\nI mean, I don't think it's like, the *only* thing I judge myself on. I care about my grades and my art, too! But... I guess if I feel like I look a little heavier or softer one day, it does kind of ruin my mood. It just makes me feel like I'm failing at being disciplined, you know? Like I'm letting myself go. \n\nBut if I feel light and empty, I just feel so much more accomplished. It's just a way of measuring if I'm staying on track with my goals, right? That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I wouldn't say I severely limit things. That sounds so... harsh, I guess? I just try to be really careful and intentional about it. 

Like, I kind of plan my day around staying focused, so I don't really need a lot of food. I'll usually just have coffee before I go to the art studio. And then... well, if I'm at the café and people are around and watching, I might have a few bites of something small, just so they don't worry. 

But I definitely stay away from things like bread or anything oily. If I eat those, it just feels like... like it would completely undo all the hard work and control I've built up. And I really don't want to lose that control. It's just about making healthy, disciplined choices, you know? That's a good way to handle things, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what's best for my body and my goals.
